Denote the event

\displaystyle\widetilde{\cal A}:=\left\{\max_{m\in\{1,2\}}h\left(\check{\bm{\sigma}}_{m},\bm{\sigma}_{m}\right)\leq\widetilde{\eta}_{1}\text{~and~}\max_{m\in\{1,2\}}h\left(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}^{[k]}_{m},\bm{\sigma}_{m}\right)\leq\widetilde{\eta}_{2}\text{~for~}k=0,1\right\}

where
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}=C\left(\log^{6}(n\vee d)\left(\bar{a}\right)^{-1}+\left(\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)\right)^{2}\left(\bar{a}\right)^{-2}\right)
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{2}=C\left(\bar{a}\right)^{-1}
, and
\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\cal A}\right)\geq 1-C\left(n\vee d\right)^{-3}
. On
\widetilde{\cal A}
, the remaining proofs are almost the same as that of alignment for network labels and hence omitted.

7.5
Proof of Theorem
4

The strategy is similar to the proof of Theorem
1
. Using the same notations there, we get

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{z}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Theta}_{\mathbf{z}}^{(\mathbf{B}_{1,2},\bm{\sigma}_{1,2})}}\ h(\widehat{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in{\mathfrak{N}}^{c}}\inf_{\widehat{z}_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{N}}|}\sum_{\mathbf{z}^{*}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\mathfrak{N}}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}_{i}\neq z_{i}^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\inf_{\widehat{z}_{i}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}^{(i)}}(\widehat{z}_{i}\neq 1)+\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}^{(i)}}(\widehat{z}_{i}\neq 2)\right),

where we define the following hypothesis testing for each
i\in[n]
:

H_{0}^{(i)}:z_{i}^{*}=1\quad v.s.\quad H_{1}^{(i)}:z_{i}^{*}=2.

It then suffices to lower bound the following probability

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right).

Define
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega):=\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)
. Then for any
\vartheta>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)>0\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}(\omega)>0\Big)=\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\prod_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}h_{\omega}(x_{\omega})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}\exp\big(\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)/2\big)}{\exp(\vartheta/2)}\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\prod_{\omega}\frac{\exp(x_{\omega}/2)h_{\omega}(x_{\omega})}{\mathbb{E}\exp\big(\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)/2\big)},

where
{\cal X}:=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d(d+1)/2}:0\leq\sum_{\omega}x_{\omega}\leq\vartheta\}
and
h_{\omega}
is the probability mass function of
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)
. By the moment generating function of Poisson variables, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\Big(\frac{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)}{2}\Big)=

\displaystyle\prod_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}e^{-\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{2}}\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\sqrt{\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\exp\Big(-\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}/2\Big)=\exp\big(-I^{\ast}/2\big)

Define

q_{\omega}(x):=\frac{\exp(x/2)h_{\omega}(x)}{\mathbb{E}\exp\big(\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)/2\big)},

which is a probability mass function for any
\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
. Let
Y_{\omega},\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
be a sequence of independent random variables such that
Y_{\omega}\sim q_{\omega}(\cdot)
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-(I^{\ast}+\vartheta)/2\big)\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}q_{\omega}(x_{\omega})=\exp\big(-(I^{\ast}+\vartheta)/2\big)\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\geq 0\Big).

Let
M_{\omega}(\cdot)
be the moment generating function of
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)
so that

\displaystyle M_{\omega}(t):=\mathbb{E}\exp\big(t\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\big)=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-t\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}-1\Big]\Big)

and

\displaystyle M_{\omega}^{\prime}(t)=\exp\Big(-t\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}-1\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\bigg(-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)

and

\displaystyle M_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}(t)=\exp\Big(-t

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}-1\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\bigg(-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-t\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big[\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}-1\Big]\Big)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big(\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\Big)^{t}\log^{2}\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}

Then
Y_{\omega}
has the moment generating function
\mathbb{E}\exp(tY_{\omega})=M_{\omega}(t+1/2)M^{-1}_{\omega}(1/2)
. Since
\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}=M_{\omega}^{\prime}(1/2)M_{\omega}^{-1}(1/2)
and
\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}^{2}=M_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}(1/2)M_{\omega}^{-1}(1/2)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}=-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\cdot\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\cdot\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)^{2}+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\cdot\log^{2}\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle{\rm Var}(Y_{\omega})=\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\cdot\log^{2}\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}.

For
\mathbf{B}_{1}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}
from the parameter space, the ratio
\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)/\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega),\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)/\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)\in[\gamma^{-1},\gamma]
for all
\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
and
\gamma
is treated as a constant. As a result,

\displaystyle{\rm Var}(Y_{\omega})\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}}\frac{\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\asymp

\displaystyle\frac{\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}

\displaystyle\asymp\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}

for all
\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
.

We now calculate the third moment of
|Y_{\omega}|
. Towards that end, let us explicitly find out the distribution of
Y_{\omega}
. For any
t=0,1,2,\cdots
, denote
\tilde{x}_{t}:=t\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)
. Then
Y_{\omega}
takes value from
\{\tilde{x}_{0},\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{2},\cdots\}
with the probability mass function

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y_{\omega}=\tilde{x}_{t})=q_{\omega}(\tilde{x}_{t})=e^{-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}\frac{\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{t}}{t!},

implying that
Y_{\omega}
is a linear transformation of
{\rm Poisson}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)
. Since Poisson distribution is sub-exponential, we have
\mathbb{E}|Y_{\omega}|^{3}<\infty
.

Now taking
\vartheta=\sqrt{\sum_{\omega}{\rm Var}(Y_{\omega})}
, by Berry-Esseen theorem, we can get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\bigg)\geq\exp\big(-(I^{\ast}+\vartheta)/2\big)\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\geq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\exp\bigg(-\frac{I^{\ast}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}{\rm Var}(Y_{\omega})}\bigg)\cdot\big(\Phi(1)-\Phi(0)\big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp\bigg(-\frac{I^{\ast}}{2}\big(1+o(1)\big)\bigg),

provided that
I^{\ast}\to\infty
.

7.6
Proof of Lemma
4

By definition,
\mathbb{I}(\widehat{z}=2)
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\delta I^{*}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left(\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\right)>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]+\delta I^{*}\Bigg),

(49)

where
\delta=o(1)
shall be determined later.

We now analyze both terms of (
58
). First notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\delta I^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\delta I^{\ast}\Big)\bigg)\cdot\mathbb{E}\exp\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\delta I^{\ast}\Big)\bigg)\cdot\exp\bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta I^{\ast}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta I^{*}\right)=\exp\bigg(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}(1-o(1))\bigg),

(50)

where the last equality holds provided that
\delta=o(1)
. Denote

\xi_{ideal}:=\mathbb{I}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\delta I^{*}\right)

Then (
7.6
) implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\xi_{ideal}\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}(1-o(1))\bigg)

(51)

as
I^{*}\rightarrow\infty
.

It suffices to show the second term of (
58
) vanishes. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left(\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\right)>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]+\delta I^{*}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left(\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}+\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]+\delta I^{*}\Bigg)

(52)

Without loss of generality, suppose that
\|\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}-\mathbf{P}_{1}\|_{\ell_{1}},\|\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}-\mathbf{P}_{2}\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}
for some
\tilde{\rho}=o(1)
. This means that for
\forall m\in[1,2]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}|\mathbf{P}_{m}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{m}(\omega)|\leq\tilde{\rho}I^{*}

This implies that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]\Bigg|\leq 2\tilde{\rho}I^{*}

Now set
\delta=\tilde{\rho}^{\epsilon}
for some fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and we get

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]\Bigg|=o\left(\delta I^{*}\right).

Then term (
7.6
) can be further bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left(\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}+\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]+\delta I^{*}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\frac{\delta}{4}I^{*}\Bigg)+\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}>\frac{\delta}{4}I^{*}\Bigg)

(53)

To control the first term of (
7.6
), we begin with:

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left|\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{|\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)|}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}(1+o(1))

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{\tilde{\rho}\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}

(54)

where in the first inequality we’ve used the following fact:

\displaystyle\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{d(d+1)/2}\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{d(d+1)/2}\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\vee\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\min_{\omega}\min_{m\in[2]}\mathbf{P}_{m}(\omega),

(55)

where the last inequality holds by the constraints in the parameter space
\mathbf{\Theta}
in (
2.1
) and the block structure of
\mathbf{P}_{m}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{m}
.

As a result, there exists an absolute constant
C>1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\frac{\delta}{4}I^{*}\Bigg)\leq\mathbb{I}\bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}/d^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\frac{\delta I^{\ast}}{4C}\bigg)

Denote the event

{\cal G}:=\left\{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}/d^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\frac{\delta I^{\ast}}{4C}\right\}.

Due to independence between
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}
and
\mathbf{X}
, we have, by Chernoff bound and conditioned on
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}
, that for any
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\cal G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\exp\bigg(\lambda\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}/d^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)>\exp\big(\lambda\delta I^{\ast}/4C\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda\delta}{4C}I^{*}\right)\prod_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\exp\bigg(\lambda\mathbf{X}(\omega)\frac{\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}/d^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda\delta}{4C}I^{*}\right)\exp\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\bigg(e^{\frac{\lambda\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}}{d^{2}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}}-1\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda\delta}{4C}I^{*}\right)\exp\big(C_{1}\lambda\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}\big)\leq\exp\Big(-\lambda\delta I^{\ast}/(8C)\Big)

(56)

By choosing
\lambda\asymp\tilde{\rho}^{-\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
so that
\lambda\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}/d^{2}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)=o(1)
, we get

\mathbb{P}({\cal G})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda\delta}{4C}I^{*}\right)\exp\big(C_{1}\lambda\tilde{\rho}I^{\ast}\big)\leq\exp\Big(-\lambda\delta I^{\ast}/(8C)\Big).

Recall
\delta=\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}
. We can choose
\lambda=4C\delta^{-1}
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\cal G}\right)\leq\exp(-I^{*}/2).

The same high probability bounds for the other term of (
7.6
) can be obtained similarly and hence omitted. Then

\displaystyle\xi_{pertub}:=

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\left(\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}+\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\Big[\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)-\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\Big]+\delta I^{*}\Bigg)

Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\xi_{pertub}\leq 4\exp\left(-I^{*}/2\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}(1-o(1))\right),

(57)

which completes the proof.

7.7
Proof of Theorem
5

We introduce extra notations for convenience. For any
\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\in[2]^{n}
and
\bm{\sigma},\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}:[d]\rightarrow[K]
, we define

h_{0}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(z_{i}\neq z^{\prime}_{i}\right)\quad{\rm and}\quad h_{0}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\left(\bm{\sigma}(j)\neq\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}(j)\right)

For each
l\in[n]
, by definition there exists some
\pi_{(-l)}\in S_{2}
and
\phi_{(-l)}\in S_{K}
such that
h_{0}\left(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z}^{(-l)},\pi_{(-l)}(\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\right)=h\left(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z}^{(-l)},\mathbf{z}^{\ast}\right)\leq\eta_{z}
and
h_{0}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}^{(-l)},\phi_{(-l)}\circ\bm{\sigma}\right)=h\left(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}^{(-l)},\bm{\sigma}^{\ast}\right)\leq\eta_{\sigma}
.

We now fix an
i\in[n]
and suppose
z_{i}^{\ast}=1
. Without loss of generality, we assume
\pi_{(-i)}=\text{Id}
and
\phi_{(-i)}=\text{Id}
. To avoid clutters of notations, we temporarily drop the superscript
(-i)
in
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{m}^{(-i)}(j_{1},j_{2})
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}^{(-i)}(k,l)
but keep in mind the independence structure between
\mathbf{X}_{i}
and other estimated parameters. The
i
-th network is mis-clustered when

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\big(\widehat{z}_{i}^{(-i)}=2\big)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\omega)\log\frac{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right).

(58)

Since
\mathbf{X}_{i}
is independent of
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{1}^{(-i)}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{2}^{(-i)}
, it suffices to apply Lemma
2
. Towards that end, we must bound the entry-wise error of
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}^{(-i)}_{1}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{P}^{(-i)}_{2}
, respectively. It thus suffices to bound the error of
\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{(-i)}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{(-i)}
, respectively, as shown in the following lemma. Its proof is almost identical to the proof of Lemma
8
except that the concentration inequalities should be replaced for Poisson random variables, which have sub-exponential tails. Note that
X-\mathbb{E}X
has a sub-exponential norm
O(1+\sqrt{\lambda})
if
X\sim{\rm Poisson}(\lambda)
. We hereby omit the proof of Lemma
11
.

Lemma 11
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
5
, there exists a large absolute constant
C>0
such that, for each
i\in[n]
, with probability at least
1-n^{-C}
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{2},\phi\in\mathfrak{S}_{K}}\max_{m\in\{1,2\}}\max_{k,l\in[K]}\left|\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}^{(-i)}(k,l)-\mathbf{B}_{\pi(m)}(\phi(k),\phi(l))\right|=\tilde{\rho}\cdot\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}

(59)

with some
\tilde{\rho}=o(1)
.

Denote the event
{\cal A}_{(-i)}:=\{\eqref{eq:initial-error-event}\text{~holds}\}
. Notice that
\mathbb{P}({\cal A}_{(-i)})\geq 1-n^{-C}
and we now proceed conditioned on
{\cal A}_{(-i)}
. Observe that, for
m\in\{1,2\}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}

\displaystyle|\mathbf{P}_{m}(\omega)-\widehat{}\mathbf{P}_{m}(\omega)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\bigg|\sum_{k,l\in[K]}\left[\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\mathbb{I}\big(\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)=(k,l)\big)-\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)=(k,l)\big)\right]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\bigg|\sum_{k,l\in[K]}\bigg[\left(\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)-\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\right)\mathbb{I}\big(\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)=(k,l)\big)\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)=\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)\big)

\displaystyle-\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\mathbb{I}\big(\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)\neq(k,l)\big)\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)\neq\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)\big)\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\omega}\max_{k,l\in[K]}\Big|\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)-\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\Big|+2K^{2}\max_{k,l\in[K]}\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\sum_{\omega}\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)\neq\bm{\sigma}_{m}(\omega)\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\rho}I^{*}+K^{2}d^{2}\eta_{\sigma}\theta_{0}\lesssim\tilde{\rho}I^{*},

where we’ve used (
59
) and
\eta_{\sigma}=o\left(\frac{I^{*}}{K^{2}d^{2}\theta_{0}}\right)
. By Lemma
2
, we conclude, conditioned on the event
{\cal A}_{(-i)}
and the event defined in (
8
), that

\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}_{i}^{(-i)}\neq z_{i}^{\ast})\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{I^{\ast}}{2}\big(1-o(1)\big)\Big)

The rest of proof is identical to that of Theorem
2
.

7.8
Proof of Theorem
6

It suffices to study the probability that
X_{i}
is mis-clustered by the rule (
23
). Without loss of generality, we study
\widehat{z}_{1}^{(-1)}
and assume that
z_{1}^{\ast}=1
. Then, by independence between
X_{1}
and
(\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)},\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}_{1}^{(-1)}=2)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(X_{1}\log\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}+(d-X_{1})\log\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big)\geq X_{1}\log\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}+(d-X_{1})\log\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(X_{1}\log\frac{\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big)}{\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big)}\geq d\log\frac{1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}}{1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}}{1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}}\bigg)^{d/2}\mathbb{E}\exp\bigg\{\frac{X_{1}}{2}\log\frac{\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big)}{\tilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}}{1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}}\bigg)^{d/2}\bigg(1-p_{1}+p_{1}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big)}{\tilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}\big(1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}\big)}\Big)^{1/2}\bigg)^{d}

implying that

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}_{1}^{(-1)}=2)=-I^{\ast}/2+d\log\bigg(1+\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{(1-p_{1})(1-p_{2})}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}\bigg)

where

\Delta:=(1-p_{1})\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{1-\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}}{1-\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}}}-\sqrt{\frac{1-p_{2}}{1-p_{1}}}\bigg)+p_{1}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}}{\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}}}-\sqrt{\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}}\bigg)

It is easy to verify that when
\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-1)}-p_{1}=o(p_{1}),\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-1)}-p_{2}=o(p_{2})
,
p_{1},p_{2}=o(1)
, and together with (
70
), we have

\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{(1-p_{1})(1-p_{2})}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}}=o(I^{\ast}/d).

As a result,

\log\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}_{1}^{(-1)}=2)=-\big(1-o(1)\big)\frac{I^{\ast}}{2}.

Therefore, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\widehat{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\big|{\cal E}_{0n}\Big]=\exp\Big\{-\big(1-o(1)\big)\frac{I^{\ast}}{2}\Big\}.

The rest of proof of the alignment and high probability bound is the same as the proof of Theorem
2
.

8
Proof of Technical Lemmas

8.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Denote
\bar{}\mathbf{X}(\omega):=\mathbf{X}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{z}(\omega)
for any
\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
. Now suppose
z^{\ast}=1
, then we get

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}>\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\lambda>0}\bigg[-\lambda\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}+\sum_{\omega}\log\bigg(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{-\lambda\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}+\sum_{\omega}\log\bigg(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\left[\sum_{\omega}\log\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}-\sum_{\omega}\log\Bigg(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}\Bigg)\right]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}}{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}+\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}

\displaystyle=-I^{*}/2

where the first inequality holds due to Markov’s inequality and in the second inequality we take
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}
. Similarly, if
z^{\ast}=2
, then the network will be mis-clustered if and only if

\displaystyle\sum_{\omega}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}>\sum_{\omega}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}

Due to the symmetry, the foregoing argument still stands without essential modification. This completes the proof.

8.2
Proof of Lemma
3

For any
\lambda>0
, The probability that
\mathbf{X}
is mis-clustered by Lloyd’s algorithm is bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\lambda\sum_{\omega}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right)\prod_{\omega}\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\lambda\sum_{\omega}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right)\prod_{\omega}\exp\left(\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\Big[e^{\lambda\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}}-1\Big]\right),

where the last equality is from the moment generating function of Poisson distribution. By taking logarithmic of both sides and setting
\lambda=1/2
, we end up with

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\big(\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)+\sum_{\omega}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=-I^{\ast}/2,

where
I^{\ast}:=\sum_{\omega}\big(\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}-\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\big)^{2}
. This completes the proof.

8.3
Proof of Lemma
7

We adopt the standard Cramer-Chernoff argument to establish the lower bound. Define
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega):=\bar{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}-D_{KL}\left(\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)||\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)\right)
. Then for any
\vartheta>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)>0\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)>0\right)=\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\prod_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}h_{\omega}(x_{\omega})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\vartheta\right)}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\prod_{\omega}\frac{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}x_{\omega}\right)h_{\omega}(x_{\omega})}{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}

(60)

where
{\cal X}:=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d(d+1)/2}:\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega}x_{\omega}\geq 0\}
and, with slight abuse of notations, we denote
x_{\omega}
the corresponding entry of
\mathbf{x}
. Here
h_{\omega}(\cdot)
is the probability mass function of
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)
, and the last inequality holds by the definition of
{\cal X}
.

Define

\displaystyle q_{\omega}(x)=\frac{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}x\right)h_{\omega}(x)}{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}.

It is readily seen that
q_{\omega}(x)\geq 0
, and
\sum_{x}q_{\omega}(x)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}=1
. Hence
q_{\omega}(\cdot)
is a probability mass function for any
\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}
. Let
Y_{\omega}
be a sequence of independent random variables such that
Y_{\omega}\sim q_{\omega}(\cdot)
, it follows from (
8.3
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\vartheta\right)}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in{\cal X}}\prod_{\omega}q_{\omega}(x_{\omega})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\omega}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\vartheta\right)}\mathbb{P}\left(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\vartheta\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\vartheta\geq\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\geq 0\right),

(61)

Let
M_{\omega}(\cdot)
be the moment generating function of
\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)
, that is, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle M_{\omega}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\exp\left(t\bar{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}-tD_{KL}\left(\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)||\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigg[1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{t\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}\bigg]e^{-t\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}},

and

\displaystyle M^{\prime}_{\omega}(t)=

\displaystyle e^{-t\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}\bigg[\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}e^{t\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}-\left(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\right)\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\bigg],

then the moment generating function of
Y_{\omega}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\left(tY_{\omega}\right)=\frac{\sum_{x}\exp(tx)\exp(\frac{1}{2}x)h_{\omega}(x)}{\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\right)}=\frac{M_{\omega}(t+\frac{1}{2})}{M_{\omega}(\frac{1}{2})}

Therefore, direct algebra gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\mathbb{E}\exp\left(tY_{\omega}\right)}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}=\frac{M^{\prime}_{\omega}(\frac{1}{2})}{M_{\omega}(\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}-\left(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\right)\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}}

\displaystyle=-\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}+\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}}{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}}

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Y^{2}_{\omega}

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{2}\exp\left(tY_{\omega}\right)}{dt^{2}}\bigg|_{t=0}=\frac{M^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}(\frac{1}{2})}{M_{\omega}(\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\log^{2}\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}+(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))\log^{2}\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}}{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}}

By direct calculation we have that

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}Y^{2}_{\omega}-\left(\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))\left[\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}\right]^{2}e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}}{\left[1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)+\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}\right]^{2}}

Notice that
\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)e^{\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}}=\mathbf{P}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}(\omega)
hence we obtain

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)\leq\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\left[\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}\right]^{2}(1+o(1)),

and

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)\geq\sqrt{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\left[\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}\right]^{2}(1-o(1)).

We now show that
\text{Var}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\right)\asymp I^{*}
.
Without loss of generality, assume
\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)>\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)
and recall that we have
\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\asymp\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)=o(1)
. Now write
p:=\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)
and
\epsilon:=\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)
. We consider two cases. Firstly if
\epsilon=o(p)
, then we have

\displaystyle\text{Var}(Y_{\omega})\asymp p\left[\log\left(1+\frac{\epsilon}{p}\right)+\log\left(1+\frac{\epsilon}{1-p-\epsilon}\right)\right]^{2}\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{p}

On the other hand we have
I_{\omega}\asymp\left(\sqrt{p+\epsilon}-\sqrt{p}\right)^{2}\asymp\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{p}
, which implies that
\text{Var}(Y_{\omega})\asymp I_{\omega}
. Secondly if
\epsilon\asymp p
, we simply have
\text{Var}(Y_{\omega})\asymp I_{\omega}\asymp p
. Hence we conclude that
\text{Var}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}\right)\asymp\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}I_{\omega}\rightarrow\infty
.

Furthermore, direct calculation gives that
|Y_{\omega}-\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}|<1
and hence
\mathbb{E}|Y_{\omega}-\mathbb{E}Y_{\omega}|^{3}<\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)
. Taking
\vartheta=\sqrt{\sum_{\omega}\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)}
in (
8.3
), by Berry–Esseen theorem we can proceed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}(\omega)\log\frac{\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega))}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)(1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega))}>\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\log\frac{1-\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)}{1-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-I^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(1\geq\frac{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}Y_{\omega}}{\sqrt{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\text{Var}\left(Y_{j_{1}j_{2}}\right)}}\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}\left(1\geq{\cal N}(0,1)\geq 0\right)-C\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\text{Var}\left(Y_{\omega}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{I^{*}}{2}(1+o(1))\right)

provided that
I^{*}\rightarrow\infty
.

8.4
Proof of Lemma
8

For notational simplicity, it suffices for us to prove the version using all samples (instead of leave-one-out estimator), i.e.,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z},\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{1},\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{2})=\text{Init}(\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle\widehat{}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\widetilde{z}_{i}=m)\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\omega)\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)=(k,l)\big)}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\widetilde{z}_{i}=m)\sum_{\omega}\mathbb{I}\big(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(\omega)=(k,l)\big)}

for any
k,l\in[K]
and
m\in\{1,2\}
. The proof for leave-one-out estimator defined in Algorithm
1
is essentially the same.
With slight abuse of notation, define

h_{0}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z_{i}\neq z^{\prime}_{i}),\quad h_{0}(\bm{\sigma},\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}):=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}(\bm{\sigma}(j)\neq\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}(j)),

for any
\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\prime}\in[2]^{n}
and
\bm{\sigma},\bm{\sigma}^{\prime}\in[K]^{d}
. For any fixed
(\mathbf{z},\{\mathbf{B}_{1},\mathbf{B}_{2}\},\{\bm{\sigma}_{1},\bm{\sigma}_{2}\})\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, let

\pi_{0}:=\argmin_{\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}h_{0}(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z},\pi(\mathbf{z}))
and
\phi_{0}:=\argmin_{\phi\in\mathfrak{S}_{K}}\max_{m\in\{1,2\}}h_{0}(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m},\phi(\bm{\sigma}_{m}))
where
\pi(\mathbf{z}):=(\pi(z_{1}),\cdots,\pi(z_{n}))
and similar for
\phi(\bm{\sigma}_{m})
.

Denote the event

{\cal A}=\left\{h_{0}(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z},\pi_{0}(\mathbf{z}))\leq\eta_{z},\max_{m\in\{1,2\}}h_{0}(\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m},\phi_{0}(\bm{\sigma}_{m}))\leq\eta_{\sigma}\right\}.

Under conditions of Theorem
2
,
\mathbb{P}({\cal A})\geq 1-C_{0}n^{-2}
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\pi_{0}
and
\phi_{0}
to be identity maps. For any
m\in\{1,2\}
and
k\in[K]
, define the following sets

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{m}^{*}:=\{i\in[n]:z_{i}=m\},\quad\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}:=\{i\in[n]:\widetilde{z}_{i}=m\}

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{m,k}^{*}:=\{j\in[d]:\bm{\sigma}_{m}(j)=k\},\quad\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}:=\{j\in[d]:\widetilde{\bm{\sigma}}_{m}(j)=k\}

\displaystyle{\cal C}^{*}_{m,k}:={\cal I}_{m}^{*}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,k}^{*},\quad\widehat{{\cal C}}_{m,k}:=\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}\otimes\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}.

Denote
n_{m}:=|{\cal I}_{m}^{*}|
and
d_{m,k}:=|{\cal S}_{m,k}^{*}|
. It is readily seen that the sets
\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}
and
\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}
have the following properties:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&n_{m}\vee|\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}|-\varpi_{1}n\leq|{\cal I}_{m}^{*}\bigcap\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}|\leq n_{m}\wedge|\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}|\\
&|{\cal I}_{m}^{*c}\bigcap\widehat{{\cal I}}_{m}|\leq\varpi_{2}n\end{split}

(62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&d_{m,k}\vee|\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}|-\omega_{1}d\leq|{\cal S}_{m,k}^{*}\bigcap\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}|\leq d_{m,k}\wedge|\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}|\\
&|{\cal S}_{m,k}^{*c}\bigcap\widehat{{\cal S}}_{m,k}|\leq\omega_{2}d\end{split}

(63)

where
\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2},\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\geq 0
and
\varpi_{1}+\varpi_{2}\leq\eta_{z},\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}\leq\eta_{\sigma}
. Define the collections of sets:

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{I}}_{m}:=\{{\cal I}\subset[n]:{\cal I}\text{~satisfying \eqref{eq:propertyI} as }\widehat{\cal I}_{m}\},\quad{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k}:=\{{\cal S}\subset[d]:{\cal S}\text{~satisfying \eqref{eq:propertyII} as }\widehat{\cal S}_{m,k}\}

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}:=\{{\cal I}\otimes{\cal S}\subset[n]\otimes[d]:{\cal I}\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{m},{\cal S}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k}\}.

It turns out that

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{I}}_{m}|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\varpi n}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{i}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{i}{n_{m}\choose i_{1}}{n-n_{m}\choose i_{2}}\leq(\varpi n+1)\left(\frac{en_{m}}{\varpi n}\right)^{\varpi n}\left(\frac{en}{\varpi n}\right)^{\varpi n}\leq\exp\left(C_{I}\varpi n\log\frac{1}{\varpi}\right).

and similarly
|{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k}|\leq\exp\left(C_{S}\omega d\log\frac{1}{\omega}\right)
for some absolute constant
C_{I},C_{S}>0
. Hence

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}|\leq|{\mathfrak{I}}_{m}|\cdot|{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k}|\leq\exp\left(C_{I}\varpi n\log\frac{1}{\varpi}+C_{S}\omega d\log\frac{1}{\omega}\right).

Now fix some
{\cal C}_{m,k}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}
. By definition,
{\cal C}_{m,k}={\cal I}_{m}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,k}
for some
{\cal I}_{m}\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{m}
and
{\cal S}_{m,k}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k}
. Denote
\widetilde{n}_{m}:=|{\cal I}_{m}|
and
\widetilde{d}_{m,k}:=|{\cal S}_{m,k}|
. Due to the property of
{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}
, we have
|\widetilde{n}_{m}-n_{m}|\leq\eta_{z}n
and
|\widetilde{d}_{m,k}-d_{m,k}|\leq\eta_{\sigma}d
. Moreover, let
{\cal E}_{m,k}
be edges in
{\cal C}_{m,k}
, which consists of independent Bernoulli random variables, where at least a fraction of

\displaystyle\frac{(\widetilde{n}_{m}-\varpi_{1}n)\cdot(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}-\omega_{1}d)(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}-\omega_{1}d+1)}{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\varpi_{1}n}{\widetilde{n}_{m}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\omega_{1}d}{\widetilde{d}_{m,k}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\omega_{1}d}{\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-4\alpha\varpi_{1})(1-2\beta K\omega_{1})^{2}

follows Bernoulli(
\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)
), at most a fraction of

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\omega_{2}d(\omega_{2}d+1)}{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}\leq(2\beta K\omega_{2})^{2}

follows Bernoulli(
p
) with
p_{m}<p<\gamma p_{m}
, at most a fraction of

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\omega_{2}d\cdot\widetilde{d}_{m,k}}{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}\leq 4\beta K\omega_{2}

follows Bernoulli(
q
) with
\gamma^{-1}q_{m}<q<q_{m}
, at most a fraction of

\displaystyle\frac{\varpi_{2}n\cdot\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}\leq 4\alpha\varpi_{2}

follows Bernoulli(
p
) with
p_{-m}<p<\gamma p_{-m}
where
p_{-m}:=p_{\{1,2\}\backslash m}
, at most a fraction of

\displaystyle\frac{\varpi_{2}n\cdot(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}/2)^{2}}{\widetilde{n}_{m}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{d}_{m,k}(\widetilde{d}_{m,k}+1)}\leq 4\alpha\varpi_{2}

follows Bernoulli(
q
) with
\gamma^{-1}q_{-m}<q<q_{-m}
with
q_{-m}:=q_{\{1,2\}\backslash m}
. Then the cardinality of
{\cal E}_{m,k}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle|{\cal E}_{m,k}|=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\cal I}_{m}\\
\omega\in{\cal S}_{m,k}\cap{\cal J}_{d}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{X}_{i}(\omega).

According to the former analysis, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}|\frac{1}{2}|{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,k}+1|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\\
y\in[0,2\beta K\eta_{\sigma}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\left[(1-y)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+y^{2}\gamma p_{m}+2yq_{m}\right]+x\left[\gamma p_{-m}+q_{-m}\right].

Given that
\eta_{\sigma}\leq c\min_{m}\frac{a_{m}-b_{m}}{a_{m}K}
for some small absolute constant
c>0
and
\eta_{z}=o\left(\frac{I^{*}}{\bar{a}}\left(\frac{n}{d}\wedge 1\right)\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\\
y\in[0,2\beta K\eta_{\sigma}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\left[(1-y)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+y^{2}\gamma p_{m}+2yq_{m}\right]+x\left(\gamma p_{-m}+q_{-m}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+x\left(\gamma p_{-m}+q_{-m}\right)

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k),\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+4\alpha\eta_{z}\left[\gamma p_{-m}+q_{-m}-\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k),\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right)\right\},

for some
\rho\rightarrow 0
. Under the same condition, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}|\frac{1}{2}|{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,k}+1|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\\
y\in[0,2\beta K\eta_{\sigma}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\left[(1-y)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+2y\gamma^{-1}q_{m}\right]

\displaystyle=\min_{x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]}(1-x)\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)=(1-4\alpha\eta_{z})\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)-\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right).

As a consequence, we have that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}|\frac{1}{2}|{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,k}+1|}\right)-\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)\right|\leq\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right).

Notice that

\displaystyle\text{Var}(|{\cal E}_{m,k}|)\leq\frac{1}{2}(d_{m,k}+\eta_{\sigma}d)(d_{m,k}+\eta_{\sigma}d-1)\left[(n_{m}+\eta_{z}n)\gamma p_{m}+\eta_{z}n\gamma p_{-m}\right]\lesssim n\left(\frac{d}{K}\right)^{2}(p_{m}+\eta_{z}p_{-m}).

By Bernstein’s inequality, we have with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\big||{\cal E}_{m,k}|-\mathbb{E}|{\cal E}_{m,k}|\big|\leq C_{0}\left({dK^{-1}\sqrt{n(p_{m}+\eta_{z}p_{-m})t}}+t\right).

Take
t=C_{I}\eta_{z}n\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}+C_{S}\eta_{\sigma}d\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}+C\log n
, then we have that with probability at least
1-n^{-C}\exp\left(-C_{I}\eta_{z}n\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}-C_{S}\eta_{\sigma}d\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k}|-\mathbb{E}|{\cal E}_{m,k}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}|\frac{1}{2}|{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,k}+1|}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{K}{\sqrt{n}d}{\sqrt{(p_{m}+\eta_{z}p_{-m})\left(n\eta_{z}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}+d\eta_{\sigma}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}+\log n\right)}}

\displaystyle+K^{2}\left(\frac{\eta_{z}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}}{d^{2}}+\frac{\eta_{\sigma}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}}{nd}+\frac{\log n}{nd^{2}}\right).

(64)

Without loss of generality, we can assume
\eta_{z}>\frac{1}{n}
since otherwise we have
\eta_{z}=0
, implying exact recovery in the initialization. Hence
n\cdot\eta_{z}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}\geq\log n
and the term
\log n
in (
8.4
) can be ignored. Also, by assumption
\eta_{z}\leq\frac{C_{3}\rho I^{*}}{\bar{a}K^{2}\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)}
, which implies that
\eta_{z}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}\leq\frac{2C_{3}\rho I^{*2}}{\bar{a}K^{2}\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)}
. Otherwise, we have
\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}\geq 2I^{*}
, or equivalently
\eta_{z}\leq\exp(-2I^{*})
, implying that a better than optimal rate has been already achieved. Together with the condition
\eta_{\sigma}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}\lesssim\frac{\rho I^{*2}}{\bar{a}K^{2}\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)^{2}}
, we conclude that the first term of of (
8.4
) is of order
O\left(\frac{\rho I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right)
.

For the second term, notice that

I^{\ast}\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\frac{\big(\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)-\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)\big)^{2}}{\mathbf{P}_{1}(\omega)\vee\mathbf{P}_{2}(\omega)}\lesssim\sum_{\omega\in{\cal J}_{d}}\frac{\bar{a}}{d(n\wedge d)}\lesssim\bar{a}\Big(1+\frac{d}{n}\Big).

As a result,

\displaystyle\eta_{z}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{3}\rho I^{*2}}{\bar{a}K^{2}\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)}\lesssim\frac{\rho I^{\ast}}{K^{2}},

\displaystyle\eta_{\sigma}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\rho I^{*2}}{\bar{a}K^{2}\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\rho I^{\ast}}{K^{2}\big(\frac{d}{n}+1\big)},

implying that
K^{2}\big(\frac{\eta_{z}}{d^{2}}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{z}}+\frac{\eta_{\sigma}}{nd}\log\frac{1}{\eta_{\sigma}}\big)=O\big(\frac{\rho I^{\ast}}{d^{2}}\big)
. Moreover, if
\frac{K^{2}\log n}{n}\to 0
, we have

K^{2}\frac{\log n}{nd^{2}}=o\Big(\frac{I^{\ast}}{d^{2}}\Big),\quad\textrm{since}\quad I^{\ast}\to\infty,

then the second term of (
8.4
) is of order
O\left(\frac{\rho^{\prime}I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right)
for some
\rho^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
.

Finally, taking a union bound over
{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}
would lead to

\displaystyle\left|\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}|\frac{1}{2}|{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,k}+1|}-\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)\right|\leq\tilde{\rho}\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right).

with probability at least
1-n^{-C}
, where
\tilde{\rho}=\rho\vee\rho^{\prime}=o(1)
.

It remains to the consider the estimation of
\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)
. To that end, we additionally define

{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k,l}:=\{{\cal I}\otimes{\cal S}\otimes{\cal S}^{\prime}\subset[n]\otimes[d]\otimes[d]:{\cal I}\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{m},{\cal S}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,k},{\cal S}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{m,l}\},

for
k\neq l\in[K]
with
|{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k}|\leq\exp\left(C_{I}\varpi n\log\frac{1}{\varpi}+2C_{S}\omega d\log\frac{1}{\omega}\right)
. Fix some
{\cal C}_{m,k,l}={\cal I}_{m}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,k}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,l}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{m,k,l}
. Let
{\cal E}_{m,k,l}
be edges between
{\cal I}_{m}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,k}
and
{\cal I}_{m}\otimes{\cal S}_{m,l}
. Analogue to the previous case, since
\eta_{z}=O\left(\rho\cdot\frac{I^{*}}{\bar{a}}\left(\frac{n}{d}\wedge 1\right)\right)
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k,l}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}||{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,l}|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\\
y\in[0,2\beta K\eta_{\sigma}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\left[(1-y)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)+2y\gamma p_{m}+2yq_{m}\right]+x\left(\gamma p_{-m}+q_{-m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l),\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)+\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right)\right\},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k,l}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}||{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,l}|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[0,4\alpha\eta_{z}]\\
y\in[0,2\beta K\eta_{\sigma}]\end{subarray}}(1-x)\left[(1-y)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,k)+2y\gamma^{-1}q_{m}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)-\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right).

We then arrive at

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{|{\cal E}_{m,k,l}|}{|{\cal I}_{m}||{\cal S}_{m,k}||{\cal S}_{m,l}|}\right)-\mathbf{B}_{m}(k,l)\right|\leq\rho\left(\frac{I^{*}}{d(d+1)/2}\right).

and

\displaystyle\text{Var}(|{\cal E}_{m,k,l}|)\leq(d_{m,k}+\eta_{\sigma}d)(d_{m,l}+\eta_{\sigma}d)\left[(n_{m}+\eta_{z}n)\gamma p_{m}+\eta_{z}n\gamma p_{-m}\right]\lesssim n\left(\frac{d}{K}\right)^{2}(p_{m}+\eta_{z}p_{-m}).

The remaining analysis is almost identical and hence omitted.

\square

We introduce the following lemma
(
Brailovskaya and van Handel 2022
, Theorem 2.13, Remark 2.14)
.

Lemma 12

(
Brailovskaya and van Handel 2022
)
.

Let
\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{Z}_{i}
where
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
’s are independent
m\times m
random matrices with
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Z}_{i}=0
. Then for a universal constant
C
and any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|\geq\sigma(\mathbf{Y})+C\left(v(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log m)^{\frac{3}{4}}+\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})t^{\frac{1}{2}}+R(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{3}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{2}{3}}t^{\frac{2}{3}}+R(\mathbf{Y})t\right)\right)\leq me^{-t}

where we define

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{Y}):=\max\left\{\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y}):=\sup_{\left\|v\right\|=\left\|w\right\|=1}\left[\mathbb{E}|\left\langle v,\mathbf{Y}w\right\rangle|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle v(\mathbf{Y}):=\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{where~}\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\in\mathbb{R}^{m^{2}\times m^{2}}\text{~and~}\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})(ij,kl)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{Y}(i,j)\mathbf{Y}(k,l)\right]

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{Y}):=\left\|\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|\mathbf{Z}_{i}\right\|\right\|_{\infty}

Decompose
\mathbf{X}_{i}=\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}+\mathbf{X}_{i}^{l}
for
i\in[n]
where
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}(j_{1},j_{2})=\mathbb{I}(j_{1}\leq j_{2})\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})
for
j_{1},j_{2}\in[d]
. Then we have
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right\|\leq\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}^{u}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}\right)\right\|+\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{l}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{l}\right)\right\|
.

We first bound
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}^{u}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}\right)\right\|
. Now we write
\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}:=\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}
for
i\in[n]
. Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1}\leq j_{2}}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})e_{j_{1}}e^{\top}_{j_{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1}\leq j_{2}\in[d]}\mathbf{Z}_{ij_{1}j_{2}}

where
\mathbf{Z}_{ij_{1}j_{2}}=\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})e_{j_{1}}e^{\top}_{j_{2}}
. To apply Lemma
12
, notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u\top}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}+\sum_{i\neq j}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u\top}\bar{}\mathbf{X}^{u}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u\top}\bar{}\mathbf{X}^{u}_{i}

where for
i\in[n]
and
j_{1},j_{2}\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u\top}\bar{}\mathbf{X}^{u}_{i}\right)(j_{1},j_{2})=\begin{cases}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}(l,j_{1})]^{2},&\quad\text{if~}j_{1}=j_{2}\\
0,&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

Hence
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}
is a diagonal matrix with
\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\|\leq nd\bar{p}
. Due to symmetry, the same bound holds for
\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right\|
and thus
\sigma(\mathbf{Y})\leq\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}
. Then we consider the
(ij,kl)
-th entry of
\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})
, which takes form of

\displaystyle\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})(ij,kl)=\mathbb{E}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n}\bar{}\mathbf{X}^{u}_{l^{\prime}}(i,j)\bar{}\mathbf{X}^{u}_{l^{\prime}}(k,l)=\begin{cases}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\bar{}\mathbf{X}_{l^{\prime}}^{u}(i,j)]^{2},&\quad\text{if~}i=k,j=l\\
0,&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

Hence
{\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})}
is a diagonal matrix with
\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\|\leq n\bar{p}
. Hence we have
\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})\leq v(\mathbf{Y})\leq\sqrt{n\bar{p}}
, where the first inequality can be found in
(
Bandeira et al. 2021
, Section 2.1)
. It suffices to notice that

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{Y})=\left\|\max_{i\in[n],j_{1},j_{2}\in[d]}\left\|\mathbf{Z}_{ij_{1}j_{2}}\right\|\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1

Lemma
12
entails that with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-4}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}^{u}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{u}\right)\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}

provided that
d\gtrsim\log^{3}(n\vee d)
and
\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}\gtrsim\log^{2}(n\vee d)
. The bound for
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}^{l}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{l}\right)\right\|
is almost identical except that the diagonal term is
0
, thus the proof is omitted.

8.5
Proof of Proposition
1

The proof consists of two parts.

Network labels recovery.

The proof can directly adapted from the result for initialization in
Jing et al. 2021
. Notice that
(
Jing et al. 2021
, Theorem 4.4)
requires
n\leq d
, which can be removed by carefully examining the proof therein. Here we just state the revised version without the proof, adapted to our notations.

Lemma 13

(Tensor incoherent norm
Jing et al. 2021
)
.

Suppose that
(n\wedge d)d\bar{p}\geq\log(n\vee d)
. Denote
m_{1}=m_{2}=d
and
m_{3}=n
. Then for
k=1,2,3
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\left\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}\right\|_{k,\delta}\geq t\}\leq\frac{2}{(n\vee d)^{4}}+10\log^{2}(n\vee d)\lceil\log_{2}\delta^{2}m_{1}\rceil\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{C_{3}\bar{p}}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{3t}{C_{4}\delta}\right)\right]

provided
t\geq\max\left\{C_{1},C_{2}\delta\sqrt{m_{k}}\log(n\vee d)\right\}\sqrt{(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log(\delta^{2}m_{k})\log(n\vee d)
for some absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4}>0
.

We also introduce the following lemma establish the concentration for sum of Bernoulli random matrices.

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
\mathbf{X}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{X}_{n}\in\{0,1\}^{d\times d}
are independent symmetric Bernoulli matrices with independent entries up to symmetry. Denote
\bar{p}:=\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\|_{\infty}
and suppose
d\geq C\log^{3}(n\vee d)
for some absolute constant
C>0
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)\right\|\geq C_{0}\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}\right)\leq 1-(n\vee d)^{-3}

provided that
\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}\geq C_{1}\log^{2}(n\vee d)
for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
.

As a result, we can essentially first follow the proof of Lemma 5.6 in
Jing et al. 2021
but substitute the proof for
\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}_{r}}\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{U}-\bar{}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{O}\right\|
with Lemma
14
(hence no log factor on the condition of
\sigma_{r}\left(n_{1}^{\ast}\mathbf{P}_{1}+n_{2}^{\ast}\mathbf{P}_{2}\right)
) and substitute the incoherent norm with Lemma
13
, and then follow the proof of the first part of
(
Jing et al. 2021
, Theorem 5.4)
to obtain that with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle h(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\leq\frac{C\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log^{2}(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)}{(n\wedge d)d\bar{p}}=\frac{C\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)}{\bar{a}}

(65)

provided that
\sigma_{r}\left(n_{1}^{\ast}\mathbf{P}_{1}+n_{2}^{\ast}\mathbf{P}_{2}\right)\gtrsim\sqrt{nd\bar{p}}
. Hence
\check{}\mathbf{z}
is a consistent estimator of
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, i.e.,
h(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})=o(1)
as long as
\bar{a}\gg\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)
.

Local memberships recovery.

Without loss of generality, we assume
h_{0}(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})=h(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})
. Denote
\mathbf{E}_{i}:=\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}
for
i\in[n]
and
\check{n}_{m}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)
for
m\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\cal A}
denote the event in (
65
) we continue on
{\cal A}
. For
m\in\{1,2\}
we have

\displaystyle\check{n}_{m}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z_{i}^{\ast}=m)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z_{i}^{\ast}\neq\check{z}_{i})\geq n_{m}^{\ast}-nh(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\geq(1-c_{0})n_{m}^{\ast}

for some constant
c_{0}\in[0,1)
, where the last inequality holds if
\bar{a}\gg\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)
. Then for
m\in\{1,2\}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i:\check{z}_{i}=m}\mathbf{X}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{m}+{\cal D}_{\mathbf{P}}+{\cal D}_{\mathbf{E}}+\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}

where
{\cal D}_{\mathbf{P}}:=(\check{n}_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{P}_{m}
and
{\cal D}_{\mathbf{E}}:=({\check{n}_{m}})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}-({n_{m}^{\ast}})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}
. First note that

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal D}_{\mathbf{P}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\left(\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{P}_{\check{z}_{i}}\right)\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m,z_{i}^{\ast}\neq m)\left(\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{P}_{\check{z}_{i}}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{nh(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})}{\check{n}_{m}}\left\|\mathbf{P}_{1}-\mathbf{P}_{2}\right\|\lesssim h(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\left\|\mathbf{P}_{1}-\mathbf{P}_{2}\right\|

Meanwhile we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal D}_{\mathbf{E}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}-\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m)-\mathbb{I}(z_{i}^{\ast}=m)\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\left(\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}-\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\right)\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}\neq m,z_{i}^{\ast}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\frac{1}{n_{m}^{\ast}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m,z_{i}^{\ast}\neq m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\frac{c_{0}}{(1-c_{0})n_{m}^{\ast}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

(66)

For the last term of (
8.5
), by Lemma
14
we have with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(z^{\ast}_{i}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{n_{m}^{\ast}d\bar{p}}

(67)

It suffices to bound the first two terms of (
8.5
). The following lemma will be needed.

Lemma 15
.

We have with probability at least
1-4(n\vee d)^{-3}
for
m\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}=m,z_{i}^{\ast}\neq m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{n(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log(n)

and the same bound also holds for
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\check{z}_{i}\neq m,z_{i}^{\ast}=m)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|
.

Using Lemma
15
and eq. (
67
), we have with probability at least
1-5(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\cal D}_{\mathbf{E}}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\bar{p}}{n}}+\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log n

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)\bar{p}}\log^{3}(n\vee d)

Collecting all pieces above, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\check{n}_{m}}\sum_{i:\check{z}_{i}=m}\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbf{P}_{m}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)\bar{p}}\log^{3}(n\vee d)+h(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\cdot d\bar{p}

with probability at least
1-5(n\vee d)^{-3}
. Denote
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{m}=\text{SVD}_{K}\left(\sum_{i:\check{z}_{i}=m}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)
and notice that
\text{col}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{m}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{m}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
span the singular space of
\mathbf{P}_{m}
(or
\mathbf{Z}_{m}
). Following the proof of first claim of Theorem 5.4 in
Jing et al. 2021
and applying Davis-Kahan theorem, we obtain that with probability at least
1-5(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle h(\check{\bm{\sigma}}_{m},\bm{\sigma}_{m})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}_{K}}\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{m}-\mathbf{Z}_{m}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{m}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{O}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(\frac{d}{n}+1\right)\bar{p}\log^{6}(n\vee d)+h^{2}(\check{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\cdot(d\bar{p})^{2}}{\sigma_{K}^{2}\left(\mathbf{P}_{m}\right)}\lesssim\frac{\log^{6}(n\vee d)}{\bar{a}}+\left(\frac{\kappa_{0}^{6}r^{3}\log(\kappa_{0}r)\log^{4}(n\vee d)}{\bar{a}}\right)^{2}

where the last inequality holds as
\sigma_{K}(\mathbf{P}_{m})=\sigma_{K}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{m}\mathbf{B}_{m}\mathbf{Z}_{m}^{\top}\right)\geq\sigma_{K}^{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{m})\sigma_{K}(\mathbf{B}_{m})\gtrsim d\bar{p}
under assumption (A1) and (A3), and the fact that
\bar{a}\gg\log^{6}(n\vee d)
and event
{\cal A}
hold. Notice that the above argument holds for
m\in\{1,2\}
and hence the proof is completed by taking a union bound on
{\cal A}
.

8.6
Proof of Lemma
10

The following lemma is modified from Lemma
12
, whose proof is deferred to Section
8
.

Lemma 16
.

Let
\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{Z}_{i}
where
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
’s are independent
m^{\prime}\times m
random matrices with
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Z}_{i}=0
. Then for a universal constant
C
and any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|\geq\sigma(\mathbf{Y})+C\left(v(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log\bar{m})^{\frac{3}{4}}+\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})t^{\frac{1}{2}}+R(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{3}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{2}{3}}t^{\frac{2}{3}}+R(\mathbf{Y})t\right)\right)\leq\bar{m}e^{-t}

where we define
\bar{m}:=\max\{m,m^{\prime}\}
,

\displaystyle v(\mathbf{Y}):=\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{where~}\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\in\mathbb{R}^{m^{\prime}m\times m^{\prime}m}\text{~and~}\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})(ij,kl)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{Y}(i,j)\mathbf{Y}(k,l)\right]

and
\sigma(\mathbf{Y}),\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y}),R(\mathbf{Y})
the same as those in Lemma
12
.

Due to the independence between
\mathbf{U}
and
\bm{\mathcal{E}}
, we temporarily view
\mathbf{U}
as fixed. Notice that
\bm{\mathcal{E}}
is slice-wise symmetric, we can decompose it as
\bm{\mathcal{E}}=\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}+\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
where
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}(j_{1},j_{2},i):=\mathbb{I}(j_{1}\leq j_{2})\left[\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})-\mathbf{P}_{z_{i}^{\ast}}(j_{1},j_{2})\right]
and
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}:=\bm{\mathcal{E}}-\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}
. Notice that both
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}
and
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
have independent entries with many zeros. Also,
\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|+\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|
.

We first consider
\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|
. For simplicity we let
\Omega_{u}:=\{j\in[d^{2}]:{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(i,j)=0,\forall i\in[n]\}
, which is fixed and
|\Omega_{u}|=\frac{d(d+1)}{2}
by construction. Denote
\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r^{2}}
with
\mathbf{A}={\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d^{2}}
and
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{d^{2}}\mathbf{A}(i,l)\mathbf{B}(l,j)e_{i}e_{j}^{\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{d^{2}}\mathbf{Z}_{il}

in form of Lemma
16
with
\mathbf{Z}_{il}=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbf{B}(l,j)\right)\mathbf{A}(i,l)e_{i}e_{j}^{\top}
and
N=nd^{2}
. It suffices to compute
\sigma(\mathbf{Y})
,
\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})
,
v(\mathbf{Y})
and
R(\mathbf{Y})
, respectively.

First of all, we consider the upper bound for
\sigma(\mathbf{Y})
. Observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\|=\left\|\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A})\mathbf{B}\right\|{\leq}\left\|\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A})\right\|

where the inequality holds since
\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}
is orthonormal. Further notice that the
(i,j)
-th entry of
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d^{2}}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbf{A}(l,i)\mathbf{A}(l,j)=\begin{cases}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(l,i)]^{2},&\quad\text{if~}i=j\in\Omega_{u}\\
0,&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

This indicates that
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{A}
is a diagonal matrix with its maximum entry bounded by
n\bar{p}
and hence
\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{n\bar{p}}
. On the other hand,
\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right\|=\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\right\|
and the
(i,j)
-th entry of
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{d^{2}}\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{d^{2}}(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top})(l_{1},l_{2})\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}(i,l_{1})\mathbf{A}(j,l_{2})=\begin{cases}\sum_{l\in\Omega_{u}}(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top})(l,l)\mathbb{E}[{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(i,l)]^{2},&\quad\text{if~}i=j\\
0,&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

Again,
\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}
is a diagonal matrix with its maximum entry bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in\Omega_{u}}(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top})(l,l)\mathbb{E}[{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(i,l)]^{2}

Observe that
|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})(i,j)|=|\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{U}(i,l)\mathbf{U}(j,l)|\leq r\delta^{2}
, hence
|(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top})(l,l)|=|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})(l,l)|\leq r^{2}\delta^{4}
. Thus
\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim r\delta^{2}d\sqrt{\bar{p}}\leq(\kappa_{0}r)^{2}\sqrt{\bar{p}}
. Hecne provided that
n\gtrsim(\kappa_{0}r)^{4}
, we have
\sigma(\mathbf{Y})\leq\sqrt{n\bar{p}}
.

Next, we consider an upper bound for
v(\mathbf{Y})
. By definition, the
(ij,kl)
-th entry of the covariance matrix
\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\in\mathbb{R}^{nr^{2}\times nr^{2}}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{ij}\mathbf{Y}_{kl}=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{d^{2}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{d^{2}}\mathbf{B}(l_{1},j)\mathbf{B}(l_{2},l)\mathbb{E}\mathbf{A}(i,l_{1})\mathbf{A}(k,l_{2})=\begin{cases}\sum_{l^{\prime}\in\Omega_{u}}\mathbf{B}(l^{\prime},j)\mathbf{B}(l^{\prime},l)\mathbb{E}[{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(i,l^{\prime})]^{2},&\quad\text{if~}i=k\\
0,&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

This implies that
\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})
admits the following block diagonal structure

\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})=\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}&11&\cdots&1r^{2}&21&\cdots&2r^{2}&\cdots&n1&\cdots&nr^{2}\\
11&&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&&\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y}(1,:))&&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&&\cdots&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}\\
1r^{2}&&&&&&&&\\
21&&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&&&\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y}(2,:))&&\cdots&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}\\
2r^{2}&&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&&\vdots&&&\vdots&&\ddots&&\vdots\\
n1&&&&&&&&\\
\vdots&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&&&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&&\cdots&&\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y}(n,:))\\
nr^{2}&&&&&&&&\\
\end{array}

For any
i\in[n]
and any
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{2}}
such that
\left\|u\right\|=\left\|v\right\|=1
, we have

\displaystyle|u^{\top}\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y}(i,:)v|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r^{2}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r^{2}}u_{l_{1}}v_{l_{2}}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{il_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{il_{2}}\right|=\left|\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r^{2}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r^{2}}u_{l_{1}}v_{l_{2}}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}_{il_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{il_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{l\in\Omega_{u}}\mathbb{E}[{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u})(i,l)]^{2}\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r^{2}}u_{l_{1}}\mathbf{B}(l,l_{1})\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r^{2}}v_{l_{2}}\mathbf{B}(l,l_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq r^{4}\delta^{4}d^{2}\bar{p}

where the inequality holds since
\max_{i,j}|\mathbf{B}(i,j)|\leq\max_{i,j,k,l}|\mathbf{U}(i,j)\mathbf{U}(k,l)|\leq\delta^{2}
. Thus we conclude that
\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y}(i,:)\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim r^{2}\delta^{2}d\sqrt{\bar{p}}
and hence
v(\mathbf{Y})=\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\kappa_{0}^{2}r^{3}\sqrt{\bar{p}}
.

Then we have the following bound for
R(\mathbf{Y})
:

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{Y})=\left\|\max_{i\in[n],l\in[d^{2}]}\left\|\mathbf{Z}_{il}\right\|\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{l\in[d^{2}]}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mathbf{B}(l,j)\right|\leq r\delta^{2}\leq\frac{(\kappa_{0}r)^{2}}{d}

Moreover, we have
\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})\leq v(\mathbf{Y})\lesssim\kappa_{0}^{2}r^{3}\sqrt{\bar{p}}
, cf.
(
Bandeira et al. 2021
, Section 2.1)
. Collecting the above bounds and using Lemma
16
, we obtain that with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma(\mathbf{Y})+C\left(v(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log n)^{\frac{3}{4}}+\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})\log^{\frac{1}{2}}(n\vee d)+R(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{1}{3}}\sigma(\mathbf{Y})^{\frac{2}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}(n\vee d)+R(\mathbf{Y})\log(n\vee d)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{n\bar{p}}+\kappa_{0}r^{\frac{3}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\bar{p}}(\log n)^{\frac{3}{4}}+\kappa_{0}^{2}r^{3}\sqrt{\bar{p}}(\log n)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\frac{(\kappa_{0}r)^{2}}{d}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}(\sqrt{n\bar{p}})^{\frac{2}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}(n\vee d)+\frac{(\kappa_{0}r)^{2}\log(n\vee d)}{d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{n\bar{p}}

with the proviso that
n/\log n\gtrsim\kappa_{0}^{4}r^{6}
and
\sqrt{nd^{2}\bar{p}}\gtrsim(\kappa_{0}r)^{2}\log^{2}(n\vee d)
.

The bound for
\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|
is almost identical and hence omitted. We conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|\leq\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{u}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|+\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}\left(\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{l}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}\otimes\mathbf{U}\right)\right\|\leq C\sqrt{n\bar{p}}

for some absolute constant
C>0
with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-3}
.

\square

8.7
Proof of Lemma
15

Without loss generality we bound the term
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\tilde{z}_{i}=1,z_{i}^{\ast}\neq 1)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|
. Consider some fixed
s\in[n]
, let
{\cal S}_{s}^{n}:=\left\{x\in\left\{0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}\right\}^{n}:\left\|x\right\|_{0}\leq s\right\}
and
E(s):=\sqrt{s}\max_{w\in{\cal S}_{s}^{n}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|
. Notice that
|{\cal S}_{s}^{n}|=\sum_{k\leq s}{n\choose k}\lesssim n^{s}
and for each
w\in{\cal S}_{s}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\max_{w\in{\cal S}_{s}^{n}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|=\max_{w\in{\cal S}_{s}^{n}}\sup_{\left\|u\right\|=\left\|v\right\|=1}\left\langle\bm{\mathcal{E}},u\otimes v\otimes w\right\rangle\leq\left\|\bm{\mathcal{E}}\right\|_{3,\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}}

We therefore obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E(s)\geq t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{w\in{\cal S}_{s}^{n}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\geq\frac{t}{\sqrt{s}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\bm{\mathcal{E}}\right\|_{3,\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}}}\geq\frac{t}{\sqrt{s}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(n\vee d)^{4}}+10\log^{2}(n\vee d)\lceil\log_{2}\delta^{2}d\rceil\left[\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{C_{3}s\bar{p}}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{3t}{C_{4}\sqrt{s}\delta}\right)\right]

for any
t
such that

t\geq\sqrt{s}\max\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log(n\vee d)\log(\delta^{2}d),C_{2}\delta\sqrt{d(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log(\delta^{2}d)\right\}.

In other words, we have the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(E(s)\geq C\sqrt{s}\delta\sqrt{d(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log(\delta^{2}d)\right)\leq(n\vee d)^{-4}

(68)

Note that (
68
) only holds for any given
s\in[n]
. Now consider
s\in[1,n]
, let
\epsilon_{j}=2^{j}
for
j=0,1,\cdots,k^{*}+1
with
k^{*}=\lfloor\log_{2}(n)\rfloor
, then
s\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{k^{*}}[\epsilon_{j},\epsilon_{j+1}]
. For any fixed
j
and
s\in[\epsilon_{j},\epsilon_{j+1}]
, we have
s\asymp\epsilon_{j}\asymp\epsilon_{j+1}
, and (
68
) holds up to change in constant
C
. Take a union bound over all
j=0,1,\cdots,k^{*}+1
, we claim that

\mathbb{P}\left(E(s)\geq C^{\prime}\sqrt{s}\delta\sqrt{d(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log(\delta^{2}d)\right)\leq\log_{2}(n)\cdot(n\vee d)^{-4}\leq(n\vee d)^{-3}

holds for any random
s\in[1,n]
. Thus we have with probability at least
1-(n\vee d)^{-3}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\tilde{z}_{i}=1,z_{i}^{\ast}\neq 1)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{nh(\tilde{}\mathbf{z},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})}\cdot\delta\sqrt{d(n\vee d)\bar{p}}\log^{2}(n\vee d)\log(\delta^{2}d)

The bound for
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\tilde{z}_{i}\neq 1,z_{i}^{\ast}=1)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|
is identical and hence omitted. The proof is completed by taking a union bound over
m\in\{1,2\}
.

8.8
Proof of Lemma
16

It suffices to extend Lemma
12
to non-square case. Without loss of generality we can assume
m^{\prime}<m
(otherwise we can apply the same argument to
\mathbf{Y}^{\top}
). Let

\displaystyle\check{}\mathbf{Y}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m},\quad\check{}\mathbf{Z}_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m},i\in[N]

i.e.,
\check{}\mathbf{Y}
