is constructed by adding
m-m^{\prime}
zero rows to
\mathbf{Y}
.
It is readily seen that
\left\|\check{}\mathbf{Y}\right\|=\left\|\mathbf{Y}\right\|
. Meanwhile, we have

\displaystyle\sigma(\check{}\mathbf{Y})=\max\left\{\left\|\mathbb{E}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\right)\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},\left\|\mathbb{E}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\right)\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}=\max\left\{\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},\left\|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}=\sigma(\mathbf{Y})

\displaystyle\sigma_{*}(\check{}\mathbf{Y})=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v,w\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
\left\|v\right\|=\left\|w\right\|=1\end{subarray}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle v,\check{}\mathbf{Y}w\right\rangle\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{m^{\prime}},w\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
\left\|\tilde{v}\right\|=\left\|w\right\|=1\end{subarray}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{v}\\
0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Y}w\\
0\end{bmatrix}\right\rangle\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathbb{R}^{m^{\prime}},w\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\\
\left\|v\right\|=\left\|w\right\|=1\end{subarray}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle v,\mathbf{Y}w\right\rangle\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sigma_{*}(\mathbf{Y})

For any
i,k\in[m^{\prime}]
and
j,l\in[m]
,
\text{Cov}(\check{}\mathbf{Y})(ij,kl)=\mathbb{E}\left[\check{}\mathbf{Y}(i,j)\check{}\mathbf{Y}(k,l)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{Y}(i,j)\mathbf{Y}(k,l)\right]
. For any

\displaystyle\text{Cov}(\check{}\mathbf{Y})(ij,kl)=\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{Y}(i,j)\mathbf{Y}(k,l)\right],&\quad i,k\in[m^{\prime}],j,l\in[m]\\
0.&\quad o.w.\end{cases}

implying that
v(\check{}\mathbf{Y})=\left\|\text{Cov}(\check{}\mathbf{Y})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}=v(\mathbf{Y})
with
\text{Cov}(\mathbf{Y})\in\mathbb{R}^{m^{\prime}m\times m^{\prime}m}
. Moreover, it is easy to verify that
R(\check{}\mathbf{Y})=R(\mathbf{Y})
. Together with Lemma
12
, the proof is completed.

\square

8.9
Proof of Lemma
17

Note that
\|\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast}\|
is equal to the spectral norm of the
(n+1)\times(n+1)
matrix
\mathbf{S}:=\big((0,\mathbf{X}^{\top}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast\top};\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast},0)\big)
. It suffices to prove the upper bound of
\|\mathbf{S}\|
. The proof is adapted from existing literature of concentration inequalities for sum of random matrices
(
Tropp 2012
;
Minsker 2017
;
Koltchinskii 2011
)
.

Denote
Y_{i}:=X_{i}-m_{i}^{\ast},i\in[n]
the centered Binomial random variables. By
Buldygin and Moskvichova 2013
,
Y_{i}
has a sub-Gaussian norm
O(\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})})
.
For notational brevity, we denote
\mathbf{V}_{i}:=\big(0,\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top};\mathbf{e}_{i},0\big)
where
\mathbf{e}_{i}
denotes the
i
-th canonical basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Now if suffices to bound

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}:=\sum_{i\in[n]}Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i},

which is a sum of independent random symmetric matrices and
\|Y_{i}\|_{\psi_{2}}=O(\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})})
. Denote
\phi(x)=e^{x}-x-1
. Following the same arguments as in
Minsker 2017
, we get

\mathbb{P}\big(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t\big)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}{\rm tr}\phi(\lambda\mathbf{S})}{\phi(\lambda t)},

where
\lambda>0
is to be determined. Moreover,

\mathbb{E}{\rm tr}\phi(\lambda\mathbf{S})\leq{\rm tr}\bigg(\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y_{i}(\omega)\mathbf{V}_{i}}\Big)-\mathbf{I}\bigg)

(69)

For each
i\in[n]
, similarly as
Koltchinskii 2011
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}+\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg(Y_{i}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{e^{\lambda\|Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}\|}-\lambda\|Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}\|-1}{\lambda^{2}\|Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}\|^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}+\lambda^{2}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\frac{e^{\lambda|Y_{i}|}-\lambda|Y_{i}|-1}{\lambda^{2}},

where we used the fact
\|\mathbf{V}_{i}\|=1
.
For any
\tau>0
and assume
\lambda\leq c_{0}\big(d/\log(1/p_{1})\big)^{-1/2}
for some small but absolute constant
c_{0}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}\exp\big\{4\lambda|Y_{i}|\big\}\leq 2
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda|Y_{i}|}-\lambda|Y_{i}|-1}{\lambda^{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{e^{\lambda\tau}-\lambda\tau-1}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}+\mathbb{E}\frac{|Y_{i}|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}\cdot\Big(e^{\lambda|Y_{i}|}-\lambda|Y_{i}|-1\Big)\mathbb{I}(|Y_{i}|\geq\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{dp_{1}\big(e^{\lambda\tau}-\lambda\tau-1\big)}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}^{1/2}|Y_{i}|^{4}}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}\mathbb{E}e^{4\lambda|Y_{i}|}\mathbb{P}^{1/4}\big(|Y_{i}|\geq\tau\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{dp_{1}\big(e^{\lambda\tau}-\lambda\tau-1\big)}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}+\frac{dp_{1}}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}\exp\Big\{-c_{1}\tau/\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}\Big\},

where the last inequality is by the tail bound of sub-exponential random variables and
c_{1}>0
is a universal constant. Therefore, there exists a universal constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}+C_{2}\bigg(\frac{\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\big(e^{\lambda\tau}-\lambda\tau-1\big)}{\lambda^{2}\tau^{2}}+\frac{dp_{1}}{\tau^{2}}\exp\Big\{-c_{1}\tau/\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}\Big\}\bigg)\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}+2C_{2}\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}\leq\exp\big\{2C_{2}\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}\big\},

where the second inequality holds if
\tau=\widetilde{C}_{0}\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}
and we choose a
\lambda\tau\leq 1
, where
\widetilde{C}_{0}>0
is a large absolute constant.

As a result, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,\omega}\log\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}}\leq 2C_{2}\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\sum_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}

Denote

\mathbf{S}_{v}:=\sum_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}n&0\\
0&\mathbf{I}_{n}\end{array}\right)

implying that
\|\mathbf{S}_{v}\|\leq n
and
{\rm tr}(\mathbf{S}_{v})=2n
. Then,

\displaystyle\exp\big(C_{3}\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\mathbf{S}_{v}\big)-\mathbf{I}\leq

\displaystyle C_{3}\lambda^{2}dp_{1}\mathbf{S}_{v}\bigg(1+\frac{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}}{2!}+\frac{(C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1})^{2}}{3!}+\cdots+\frac{(C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1})^{k}}{(k+1)!}+\cdots\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{S}_{v}}{n}\cdot\Big(e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}}-1\Big)

and thus

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\bigg(\exp\Big(\sum_{i}\log\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda Y_{i}\mathbf{V}_{i}}\Big)-\mathbf{I}\bigg)\leq{\rm tr}(\mathbf{S}_{v}/n)\cdot\Big(e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}}-1\Big)\leq 2\Big(e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}}-1\Big).

Continuing from (
69
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t\big)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\phi(\lambda t)}\cdot e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}}=\frac{2e^{\lambda t}}{\phi(\lambda t)}\cdot e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}-\lambda t}\leq 2\Big(1+\frac{6}{\lambda^{2}t^{2}}\Big)e^{C_{3}n\lambda^{2}dp_{1}-\lambda t}.

By minimizing the exponent w.r.t.
\lambda
and with the constraint
\lambda\leq c_{0}\big(d/\log(1/p_{1})\big)^{-1/2}
, we set
\lambda:=\min\big\{t/(2C_{3}ndp_{1}),\ c_{0}\big(d/\log(1/p_{1})\big)^{-1/2}\big\}
and get

\mathbb{P}\big(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t\big)\leq 2\Big(1+\frac{6}{\lambda^{2}t^{2}}\Big)\exp\bigg\{-\Big(-\frac{t^{2}}{4C_{3}ndp_{1}}\bigwedge\frac{t}{\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}}\Big)\bigg\}

The above exponential term is meaningful only when
t\geq C_{4}\sqrt{ndp_{1}}
and
t\geq C_{4}\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}
for some large constants , in both cases, we have
6/(\lambda^{2}t^{2})\leq 6
. As a result, we conclude that, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t\big)\leq 14\exp\bigg\{-\Big(-\frac{t^{2}}{4C_{3}ndp_{1}}\bigwedge\frac{t}{\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}}\Big)\bigg\},

which concludes the proof.

8.10
Proof of Lemma
5

We begin with K-means initial clustering.

K-means clustering.

Clearly, the K-means clustering error, described by the Hamming distance
h(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z}^{(-i)},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})
, depends on an upper bound of
\|\mathbf{X}^{(-i)}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast(-i)}\|
. It suffices to bound
\|\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast}\|
.

By
Buldygin and Moskvichova 2013
, the centered Binomial random variable
X_{i}-dp_{z_{i}^{\ast}}
is sub-Gaussian with a sub-Gaussian norm at the order of
\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}
. The following lemma characterizes a sharp upper bound of
\|\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast}\|
, whose proof is relegated to the appendix.

Lemma 17
.

There exists a large absolute constant
C>0
such that for any
t>0

\mathbb{P}\big(\|\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast}\|\geq t\big)\leq 14\exp\bigg\{-\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{4C_{3}ndp_{1}}\bigwedge\frac{t}{\sqrt{d/\log(1/p_{1})}}\Big)\bigg\}

By Lemma
17
, we get

\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{m}^{\ast}\|\leq C_{\gamma}\sqrt{ndp_{1}+d/\log(1/p_{1})}\Big)\geq 1-10^{-\gamma},

where
C_{\gamma}>0
is a large but absolute constant. We denote
{\cal E}_{0}
the above event.
By a standard analysis of K-means clustering error, conditioned on
{\cal E}_{0}
, for a small absolute constant
c_{0}>0
, we get

\max_{i\in[n]}h(\widetilde{}\mathbf{z}^{(-i)},\mathbf{z}^{\ast})\leq\eta_{z}:=C_{1}\bigg(\frac{p_{1}}{d(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}+\frac{1}{nd(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\log(1/p_{1})}\bigg)\leq c_{0},

if
d(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\geq C_{\gamma}p_{1}
and
nd(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\log(1/p_{1})\geq C_{\gamma}
for some large absolute constant
C_{\gamma}
depending on
c_{0}
or
\gamma
only.

By definition, if
p_{1}\asymp p_{2}=o(1)
and
dp_{1}\gg 1
, we have

I^{\ast}:=-2d\log\Big[\sqrt{(1-p_{1})(1-p_{2})+p_{1}p_{2}}\Big]\asymp d\frac{(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}{p_{1}},

implying that
p_{1}\wedge p_{2}=\Omega(I^{\ast}/d)
.

Following the same proof as Lemma
8
, if
\eta_{z}=o\big(I^{\ast}/(d|p_{1}-p_{2}|)\big)
and
\eta_{z}\log(1/\eta_{z})=o\big(I^{\ast 2}/(dp_{1})\big)
, we get with probability at least
1-n^{-2}
such that

\max_{i\in[n]}|\widetilde{p}_{1}^{(-i)}-p_{1}|+|\widetilde{p}_{2}^{(-i)}-p_{2}|=o\Big(\frac{I^{\ast}}{d}\Big).

(70)

Here, for ease of notations, we simply assume that
\tilde{}\mathbf{z}^{(-i)}
is already properly aligned with
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
without considering the possible permutation in
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
.

Method of moments.

Denote
M_{1}=(p_{1}+p_{2})/2
and
M_{2}=(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2})/2
. By definition of
\widehat{p}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{p}_{1}-p_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}\big|+\frac{|\widehat{M}_{2}-M_{2}|+|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}||\widehat{M}_{1}+M_{1}|}{\sqrt{M_{2}-M_{1}^{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}|+\frac{2}{|p_{1}-p_{2}|}\cdot\Big[|\widehat{M}_{2}-M_{2}|+|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}||\widehat{M}_{1}+M_{1}|\Big]

It suffices to upper bound
|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}|
and
|\widehat{M}_{2}-M_{2}|
. Let us fix
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
as well as
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
. By Chernoff bound,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{|n_{1}-n/2|\leq C\sqrt{n\log n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{|n_{2}-n/2|\leq C\sqrt{n\log n}\Big\}\Big)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(71)

Conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we write

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{1}-\frac{n_{1}p_{1}+n_{2}p_{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nd}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-dp_{1})+\frac{1}{nd}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=2}(X_{i}-dp_{2})

\displaystyle=_{\rm d}\frac{Y_{1}-n_{1}dp_{1}}{nd}+\frac{Y_{2}-n_{2}dp_{2}}{nd},

where
Y_{1}\sim{\rm Bin}(n_{1}d,p_{1})
and
Y_{2}\sim{\rm Bin}(n_{2}d,p_{2})
. By the concentration of Binomial random variables,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{|Y_{1}-n_{1}dp_{1}|\leq C\sqrt{n_{1}dp_{1}\log n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{|Y_{2}-n_{2}dp_{2}|\leq C\sqrt{n_{2}dp_{2}\log n}\Big\}\Big)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(72)

By (
71
) and (
72
), we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}\Big|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}\log n}{nd}}+Cp_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\geq 1-2n^{-2}.

Similarly, conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we write

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{2}-\frac{n_{1}p_{1}^{2}+n_{2}p_{2}^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nd(d-1)}\Bigg[\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\big(X_{i}^{2}-X_{i}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\big)+\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=2}\big(X_{i}^{2}-X_{i}-d(d-1)p_{2}^{2}\big)\Bigg]

Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\big(X_{i}^{2}-X_{i}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\big)=_{\rm d}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\bigg[\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq j_{2}\leq d}Z_{i,j_{1}}Z_{i,j_{2}}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\bigg],

(73)

where
Z_{i,j},j\in[d]
are i.i.d.
{\rm Bern}(p_{1})
random variables. The RHS of (
73
) is a sum of symmetric order-
2
U-statistics. By the classical de-coupling technique of U-statistics (see, e.g.,
(
De la Pena and Giné 2012
, Theorem 3.4.1)
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Bigg|\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\bigg[\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq j_{2}\leq d}Z_{i,j_{1}}Z_{i,j_{2}}-

\displaystyle d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\bigg]\Bigg|>t\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Bigg|\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\bigg[\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq j_{2}\leq d}Z_{i,j_{1}}\widetilde{Z}_{i,j_{2}}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\bigg]\Bigg|>t\Bigg)

(74)

for any
t>0
and
\widetilde{Z}_{i,j}
’s are independent copies of
Z_{i,j}
’s. It thus suffices to bound the RHS of (
74
), where the summation can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}

\displaystyle\bigg[\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq j_{2}\leq d}Z_{i,j_{1}}\widetilde{Z}_{i,j_{2}}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=_{\rm d}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\Big(X_{i}\widetilde{X}_{i}-d^{2}p_{1}^{2}\Big)-\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(Z_{i,j}\widetilde{Z}_{i,j}-p_{1}^{2}),

(75)

where
\widetilde{X}_{i}
is an independent copy of
X_{i}
. We only show the upper bound of the first term in the RHS of (
75
), since the second term can be bounded in the same fashion.

We first fix
\widetilde{X}_{i}
’s and upper bound
\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-dp_{1})\widetilde{X}_{i}
. Conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
and
\widetilde{X}_{i}
’s, for any
\lambda>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\exp\bigg(\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-dp_{1})\tilde{X}_{i}\lambda\bigg)=

\displaystyle e^{-dp_{1}\lambda\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\widetilde{X}_{i}}\prod_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda\widetilde{X}_{i}X_{i}}\leq e^{-dp_{1}\lambda\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\widetilde{X}_{i}}\prod_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}e^{dp_{1}(e^{\lambda\widetilde{X}_{i}}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-dp_{1}\lambda\widetilde{S}_{1}}\cdot e^{dp_{1}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(e^{\lambda\widetilde{X}_{i}}-1)},

where
\widetilde{S}_{1}:=\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\widetilde{X}_{i}
. Therefore, for any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-dp_{1})\widetilde{X}_{i}\geq

\displaystyle tdp_{1}\widetilde{S}_{1}\bigg)\leq\min_{\lambda>0}e^{-(1+t)\lambda dp_{1}\widetilde{S}_{1}+dp_{1}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(e^{\lambda\widetilde{X}_{i}}-1)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda=\widetilde{X}_{\max}^{-1}\ln(1+t)}}{{\leq}}\exp\bigg(-dp_{1}(1+t)\ln(1+t)\frac{\widetilde{S}_{1}}{\widetilde{X}_{\max}}+dp_{1}t\frac{\widetilde{S}_{1}}{\widetilde{X}_{\max}}\bigg),

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{e^{t}}{(1+t)^{1+t}}\Big)^{dp_{1}\widetilde{S}_{1}/\widetilde{X}_{\max}}\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{t^{2}dp_{1}\widetilde{S}_{1}/\widetilde{X}_{\max}}{3}\bigg)

where
\widetilde{X}_{\max}:=\max_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\widetilde{X}_{i}
and we used the fact
(1+t)^{a}-1\leq at
for
a\leq 1
and
t\in(0,1)
. A left tail can be similarly established. Conditioned on
\widetilde{X}_{i}
and
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we get

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-dp_{1})\widetilde{X}_{i}\bigg|\leq C\sqrt{dp_{1}\widetilde{S}_{1}\widetilde{X}_{\max}\log n}\bigg)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(76)

By the concentration property of Binomial random variables, conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big\{|\widetilde{S}_{1}-n_{1}dp_{1}|\leq C\sqrt{n_{1}dp_{1}\log n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{\widetilde{X}_{\max}\leq Cdp_{1}\log n\Big\}\bigg)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(77)

A similar bound can be derived for
dp_{1}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}}(\widetilde{X}_{i}-dp_{1})
can be derived by the concentration of Binomial random variables. By (
76
) and (
77
), we have

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\Big(X_{i}\widetilde{X}_{i}-d^{2}p_{1}^{2}\Big)\bigg|\leq C\sqrt{dp_{1}n_{1}dp_{1}dp_{1}\log^{2}n}\bigg)\geq 1-2n^{-2},

(78)

which, together with (
73
)-(
75
), implies that

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\big(X_{i}^{2}-X_{i}-d(d-1)p_{1}^{2}\big)\bigg|\leq C\sqrt{dp_{1}n_{1}dp_{1}dp_{1}\log^{2}n}\bigg)\geq 1-6n^{-2}.

Finally, we conclude that with probability at least
1-13n^{-2}
,

\big|\widehat{M}_{2}-M_{2}\big|\leq Cp_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}+C\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}^{3}\log^{2}n}{nd}}.

By summarizing all the above results, we can conclude that
|\widehat{p}_{1}-p_{1}|+|\widehat{p}_{2}-p_{1}|=o(I^{\ast}/d)
with probability at least
1-14n^{-2}
if

I^{\ast}\gg 1+\frac{p_{1}}{|p_{1}-p_{2}|}\sqrt{\frac{dp_{1}\log n(dp_{1}+\log n)}{n}}.

8.11
Proof of Lemma
6

The proof of K-means is identical to that of Lemma
5
by noticing that
{\rm Poisson}(\theta_{1})
has sub-exponential norm upper bounded by
O(1+\sqrt{\theta_{1}})
.

Method of moments.

Denote
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}:=M_{1}+2M_{\tiny 1/2}\sqrt{M_{1}-M_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}}
. Since
M_{\tiny 1/2}=(\theta_{1}^{1/2}+\theta_{2}^{1/2})/2+O(\theta_{1}^{-1/2})
and
\sqrt{\theta_{1}}-\sqrt{\theta_{2}}\gg 1
, we can get

\big|\widetilde{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}\big|=O\Big(\frac{\sqrt{\theta_{1}}}{\sqrt{\theta_{1}}-\sqrt{\theta_{2}}}\Big)=O\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}\Big)=o(I^{\ast}),

where the last inequality is by the condition on
I^{\ast}
.

By definition of
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\widetilde{\theta}_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}\big|+2\Big|M_{\tiny 1/2}\sqrt{M_{1}-M_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}}-\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}\sqrt{\widehat{M}_{1}-\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}|+2M_{\tiny 1/2}\frac{|M_{1}-\widehat{M}_{1}|+\big|M_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}-\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}\big|}{M_{1}-M_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}}

\displaystyle+2\big|\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}-M_{\tiny 1/2}\big|\sqrt{\widehat{M}_{1}-\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}^{2}}

It suffices to upper bound
|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}|
and
|\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}-M_{\tiny 1/2}|
. Let us fix
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
as well as
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
. By Chernoff bound,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{|n_{1}-n/2|\leq C\sqrt{n\log n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{|n_{2}-n/2|\leq C\sqrt{n\log n}\Big\}\Big)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(79)

Conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we write

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{1}-\frac{n_{1}\theta_{1}+n_{2}\theta_{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(X_{i}-\theta_{1})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=2}(X_{i}-\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=_{\rm d}\frac{Y_{1}-n_{1}\theta_{1}}{n}+\frac{Y_{2}-n_{2}\theta_{2}}{n},

where
Y_{1}\sim{\rm Poisson}(n_{1}\theta_{1})
and
Y_{2}\sim{\rm Poisson}(n_{2}\theta_{2})
. By the concentration of Poisson random variables, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{|Y_{1}-n_{1}\theta_{1}|\leq C\sqrt{n_{1}\theta_{1}\log n+\log^{2}n}\Big\}\bigcap\Big\{|Y_{2}-n_{2}\theta_{2}|\leq C\sqrt{n_{2}\theta_{2}\log n+\log^{2}n}\Big\}\Big)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(80)

By (
79
) and (
80
), we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}\Big|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{1}\log n}{n}}+C\frac{\log n}{n}\Big)\geq 1-2n^{-2}.

If
n\theta_{1}\geq C\log n
, then
|\widehat{M}_{1}-M_{1}|\leq cM_{1}
in the above event for some small constant
c>0
. Since
\theta_{1}\gg 1
, it only requires
n\geq C\log n
.

Similarly, conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
, we write

\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}-\frac{n_{1}\mu_{1}+n_{2}\mu_{2}}{n}=\frac{1}{n}\Bigg[\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\big(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{1}\big)+\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=2}\big(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{2}\big)\Bigg],

(81)

where
\mu_{1}=\mathbb{E}\sqrt{{\rm Poisson}(\theta_{1})}
and
\mu_{2}=\mathbb{E}\sqrt{{\rm Poisson}(\theta_{2})}
.

By Chapter 6 (Exercise 6.12) of
Boucheron et al. 2013
, if
X\sim{\rm Poisson}(\theta)
, then for any
\lambda\in(0,1/2)
,

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\exp\big(\lambda(\sqrt{X}-\mathbb{E}\sqrt{X})\big)\leq\lambda(e^{\lambda}-1)\mathbb{E}(X)\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{4X+1}\leq

\displaystyle\lambda(e^{\lambda}-1)\theta\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{X+1}=\lambda(e^{\lambda}-1)\theta\frac{1-e^{-\theta}}{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda(e^{\lambda}-1).

It implies that
\sqrt{X}-\mathbb{E}\sqrt{X}
is sub-exponential with a sub-exponential norm bounded by
O(1)
. Meanwhile, its variance is also bounded by
O(1)
.
By the concentration of sum of independent sub-exponential random variables, we conclude that conditioned on
\mathbf{z}^{\ast}
,

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\big(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{1}\big)\Big|\leq C\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\bigcap\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=2}\big(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{2}\big)\Big|\leq C\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\geq 1-n^{-2}.

(82)

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{1})\geq nt\Big)\leq\min_{\lambda>0}e^{-\lambda nt}\prod_{i:z_{i}^{\ast}=1}\mathbb{E}\exp\big(\lambda(\sqrt{X_{i}}-\mu_{1})\big)

Putting together (
79
)-(
82
), we get

\mathbb{P}\Big(|\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}-M_{\tiny 1/2}|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{1}\log n}{n}}+C\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\geq 1-2n^{-2}.

(83)

Recall that
M_{\tiny 1/2}\asymp\sqrt{\theta_{1}}
. Therefore,
|\widehat{M}_{\tiny 1/2}-M_{\tiny 1/2}|\leq c_{1}M_{\tiny 1/2}
if
n\theta_{1}\geq C_{1}\log^{2}n
for some small constant
c_{0}>0
, which can easily satisfied since
\theta_{1}\gg 1
and
n\geq C_{1}\log^{2}n
.

Finally, we conclude that with probability at least
1-2n^{-2}
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\widetilde{\theta}_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C\bigg(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\big(\sqrt{\theta_{1}}-\sqrt{\theta_{2}}\big)^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\theta_{1}}-\sqrt{\theta_{2}}}{2}\bigg)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{1}\log n+\log^{2}n}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{\big(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\big)^{2}}+\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\sqrt{\theta_{1}}}\bigg)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{1}\log n+\log^{2}n}{n}}.

Therefore, in the same event, we have
\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta_{1}\|=o(I^{\ast})
if

I^{\ast}\gg\frac{\theta_{1}}{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}+\bigg(\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{\big(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\big)^{2}}+\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\sqrt{\theta_{1}}}\bigg)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\theta_{1}\log n+\log^{2}n}{n}}
